@@ -32,9 +32,19 @@ impl WreqTransport {
     /// show) — if this fails to compile after a dependency bump, check
     /// `wreq_util::Emulation`'s available variants and adjust.
     pub fn new_chrome() -> Result<Self, TransportError> {
+        // cookie_store(false): twr sends a full explicit `Cookie:` header
+        // on every request (Method C paste, code-226 gate). Letting wreq
+        // ALSO jar cookies means Set-Cookie responses (e.g. rotated guest
+        // tokens, cf_clearance refreshes) silently append/override the
+        // explicit header on later requests in the same process — a
+        // stateful mutation of auth material the caller cannot see. The
+        // caller owns cookies; the transport must be stateless.
+        // (Live-found 2026-09-20: CreateList 214'd through this transport
+        // while succeeding via curl with byte-identical vars/features —
+        // cookie-jar interference is the prime suspect.)
         let client = Client::builder()
             .emulation(Emulation::Chrome131)
-            .cookie_store(true)
+            .cookie_store(false)
             .build()
             .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("failed to build wreq client: {e}")))?;

Now I understand the context fully. The `first` variable is a GraphQL error object from `payload.get("errors").and_then(|e| e.as_array()).first()`. It's a `serde_json::Value`. 

The `is_sub_selection_error(first)` function needs to check whether the error is a sub-selection error - i.e., an error whose `path` points away from the mutation root. Based on the comment:
- The error path would be something like `list.default_banner_media_results.result`
- The mutation root would be `create_list` or similar

So `is_sub_selection_error` should check that:
1. The error has a `path` field that is an array
2. The path does NOT start with the mutation root (i.e., it's a non-mutation sub-selection)

Actually, looking more carefully at the comment: "When the mutation demonstrably landed (created id present) and the error path points away from the mutation root, report success with the server warning attached."

The mutation root for CreateList would be `create_list`. The error path `list.default_banner_media_results.result` points to a sub-selection that is NOT the mutation root.

So `is_sub_selection_error` should check that the error's `path` field exists and its first element is NOT the mutation operation name (like `create_list`, `delete_list`, etc.).

Wait, but actually the simplest interpretation is: an error with a `path` field whose root is NOT the top-level mutation. In GraphQL, the `path` field in an error is an array of path components. For a mutation error that affects the whole mutation, the path might be `["create_list"]` or `null`.
```rust
/// True when a GraphQL error targets a sub-selection rather than the
/// mutation root — i.e., its `path` either (a) is absent/null (we can't
/// classify, so treat as non-sub-selection) or (b) does NOT begin with the
/// mutation root field. Used by the partial-data success rule: when the
/// mutation landed (id present) but only a banner/non-critical sub-field
/// errored, that is a warning, not a rejection.
fn is_sub_selection_error(error: &serde_json::Value) -> bool {
    let path = match error.get("path") {
        Some(p) => p,
        None => return false,
    };
    let path_arr = match path.as_array() {
        Some(a) => a,
        None => return false,
    };
    if path_arr.is_empty() {
        return false;
    }
    // A sub-selection error has a multi-segment path that does NOT start
    // at the mutation root. The canonical 214 case (live-found 2026-09-20)
    // is `list.default_banner_media_results.result` — code 214, a banner
    // media decode hiccup, not an auth/variable error.
    // We accept any error whose path descends into a sub-field: the
    // mutation root field itself is always a single-segment path
    // (e.g. ["createList"]). A path of length > 1 is a sub-selection.
    path_arr.len() > 1
        || matches!(
            path_arr.first().and_then(|v| v.as_str()),
            // Known non-mutation-root first segments for sub-selection errors
            Some("list") => true,
            _ => path_arr.len() > 1,
        )
}
```

8: ## Toolchain / build environment (this dev machine)
9: 
10: - **`crates/twr-client` needs a working native toolchain for wreq's BoringSSL
11:   linkage** (via `boring-sys`): `cmake` (installed via `winget install
12:   Kitware.CMake`) + `nasm` (winget's `NASM.NASM` package silently failed to
13:   register — `winget list` shows nothing installed despite a "Successfully
14:   installed" message; worked around by downloading the official zip release
15:   directly to `C:\Users\ADMIN\tools\nasm\nasm-2.16.03\nasm.exe` and adding it
16:   to `PATH` for the shell session) + **`libclang`/LLVM** (needed by
17:   `bindgen` to generate BoringSSL FFI bindings — `winget install LLVM.LLVM`
18:   was kicked off but not confirmed working as of this note).
19: - **Practical consequence**: `cargo build --workspace` / `cargo test
20:   --workspace` do NOT currently work end-to-end on a fresh checkout of this
21:   repo without first fixing this chain. Until this is resolved (or documented
22:   as a first-run setup step / addressed via a CI Docker image with the full
23:   toolchain baked in), always scope `cargo build/test/clippy` to the specific
24:   crates you're touching, e.g.:
25:   `cargo build -p twr-model -p twr-tx -p twr-core -p twr-auth` (these four
26:   have zero native-toolchain dependencies and build cleanly everywhere).
27: - **Action item for whoever picks this up next**: either (a) get LLVM
28:   properly installed and confirm `cargo build -p twr-client` links end to
29:   end, or (b) reconsider the plan's transport choice — the `HttpTransport`
30:   trait (crates/twr-client/src/lib.rs) was specifically designed so a
31:   `CurlImpersonateTransport` (subprocess-based, no BoringSSL linking needed
32:   in-process) can be swapped in without touching call sites, per plan §11
33:   risk #3. If native-toolchain friction turns out to be a recurring
34:   contributor-onboarding problem, that fallback might deserve to become the

70:   rendering) indexes by Rust `char`, matching the Python original's behavior
71:   of indexing by Python `str` code points — but Draft.js's own `offset`/
72:   `length` values are defined in **UTF-16 code units**. Both this port and
73:   the Python original inherit the same latent bug for non-BMP characters
74:   (emoji, some CJK) in article text; this was a pre-existing upstream
75:   limitation, not something introduced here, but worth fixing properly (UTF-16
76:   offset translation) if article rendering on emoji-heavy content ever comes
77:   up as a real bug report.
78: 
79: ## P4 (official API v2 backend) — deferred, needs human decisions first
80: 
81: Beads `twitter_cli-5o3.6.1` (OAuth2), `6.2` (dual-backend routing), `6.3`
82: (video upload) were NOT implemented in the autonomous pass because each
83: needs an input only a human can provide:
84: 
85: 1. **X developer account + app credentials.** OAuth2 user-context requires a
86:    real X developer app (client ID/secret, callback URL, approved access
87:    tier). No agent can or should create that.
88: 2. **Backend-choice confirmation.** PR #31's dual-backend shape (which
89:    commands route where, `--backend` default) is a product decision with
90:    ban-risk trade-off implications (cookie vs. official API) that SKILL.md
91:    must then document honestly.
92: 3. **Video fixtures.** Cookie-backend video (PR #41 pattern) also needs a
93:    throwaway account to verify INIT/APPEND/FINALIZE + STATUS polling.
94: 
95: When those exist, 6.1→6.2→6.3 implement in order behind
96: `--backend api-v2` (feature-gated, cookie backend stays default).
97:

101: and DM `.5.1/.5.2/.5.4` are CLOSED. Still OPEN and needing human input:
102: P6.3 list writes (`.3.1`–`.3.7`, X error 214 — see below), `dm-send`
103: (`.5.3`, needs a solicited live send to prove the wire shape), and the top
104: epics (`.3`, `.5`, `o1l`) which close only when their children do.
105: 
106: `COMPREHENSIVEPLANFORTWITTERCLI.md` §13 has the full spec. Read-only
107: additions (mentions/notifications/user-media/user-replies/lists/list-members/
108: bookmark-folders), engagement mutations (mute/block/pin), and list management
109: (create/edit/delete/add-member/follow/pin) are code-shipped. Sequencing and

Agent-first CLI for X/Twitter

Usage: twr [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  status              Auth gate — always call this first
  schema              Print the envelope schema for every command's `type`
  commands            List available commands and their envelope types
  query-ids           Show resolved query IDs per operation (baseline/cache/pinned)
  doctor              Run health checks: AUTH / CONFIG / QUERY_ID / TX_ID / TLS
  login               Manage credentials: login/logout/guide
  logout              
  cache-search        Search the local SQLite cache (FTS5, offline)
  watch               Watchlist management
  completions         Print shell completions (script to stdout, instructions to stderr)
  mcp                 Serve the tool catalog over stdio (JSON-RPC MCP shape)
  post                Post a tweet (needs --apply; --dry-run to preview)
  reply               Reply to a tweet
  quote               Quote a tweet
  edit                Edit a tweet's text (Premium-gated server-side; same CreateTweet op + edit_options, NOT a separate mutation — see bead o1l.4.2)
  dm-list             List your DM conversations (1.1 REST, own model — see SKILL.md §6)
  dm-read             Read a DM conversation's message history
  dm-send             Send a DM. HIGHEST-SCRUTINY write in twr — requires --policy write explicitly (--policy engagement does NOT cover it), its own daily cap (TWR_DM_DAILY_BUDGET, default 10), no batch flag ever. Read SKILL.md §6 before using this for anything but a solicited reply
  delete              Delete a tweet (always previews first, even with --apply)
  like                Like a tweet (alias: favorite) [alias: favorite]
  unlike              Unlike (alias: unfavorite) [alias: unfavorite]
  retweet             Retweet
  unretweet           Unretweet
  bookmark            Bookmark [alias: favorite-board]
  unbookmark          Unbookmark (alias: unbookmark alias `unfavorite-board`) [alias: unfavorite-board]
  follow              Follow a user id
  unfollow            Unfollow a user id
  mute                Mute a user id (self-only, invisible to the target)
  unmute              Unmute a user id
  block               Block a user id (target-visible: they can detect it; also triggers X's own implicit unfollow-on-block — the response may say so)
  unblock             Unblock a user id
  pin                 Pin a tweet to your profile (X allows only ONE pinned tweet at a time — pinning a second tweet REPLACES the previous pin, it does not error; re-pinning the already-pinned tweet is a no-op success)
  unpin               Unpin your profile tweet (no-op success when nothing is pinned)
  list-create         Create a list (write-tier only). Surfaces the new list_id in the envelope so an agent can script create-then-add-members
  list-edit           Rename/redescribe/toggle-private a list (write-tier only). Resends all fields (partial-update semantics unconfirmed — see write.rs)
  list-delete         Delete a list (write-tier only, destructive — always previews first even with --apply, like tweet delete; deleting a nonexistent list reports not-found exit 3, and a delete-retry on an already-deleted list is a success, not a failure)
  list-add-member     Add ONE user to a list (engagement-tier; no batch flag — bulk adds are a spam-report vector, loop in the orchestrator instead)
  list-remove-member  Remove ONE user from a list (engagement-tier)
  list-follow         Follow (subscribe to) someone else's list (engagement-tier)
  list-unfollow       Unfollow (unsubscribe from) a list (engagement-tier)
  list-pin            Pin a list to your sidebar (engagement-tier, cosmetic; the pinned- list limit is unconfirmed — documented when live-verified)
  list-unpin          Unpin a list from your sidebar (engagement-tier, cosmetic)
  feed                Home/feed timeline
  bookmarks           Bookmarks (own account)
  search              Search with the full operator flags
  tweet               Single tweet by ID or URL
  show                Show the Nth item of the last list (`~/.twr/last.json`)
  article             Long-form article by tweet ID or URL
  list                Tweets in a list timeline
  lists               Lists owned by a user id, or (with --member-of) lists they are a member of. Defaults to the authenticated account when no id is given (resolved via account/settings.json, same as bird's getCurrentUser); pass an explicit id to inspect another account
  list-members        Members of a list timeline
  mentions            Mention notifications (tweets that mention you)
  notifications       All notifications (likes/retweets/follows/quotes on your content)
  user                User profile by handle
  user-posts          Recent posts by handle
  user-replies        Posts + replies by handle (ungated replies-only tab)
  user-media          Photo/video-only posts by handle (server-side filtered tab)
  likes               Own-account likes (X restricts this to self)
  followers           Followers of a user id
  following           Accounts a user id follows
  headlines           Today's headlines (trending-search fallback until the Search Navigation surface is reverse-engineered; issue #47)
  future              Run a read command once after a delay (one-shot, NOT cron)
  help                Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --json                       Machine output as JSON [env: TWR_JSON=]
      --yaml                       Machine output as YAML (auto when piped without a flag) [env: TWR_YAML=]

READcrates/twr/src/cli/write.rs
Read 1/1 file, 587 lines
1: //! Write-command execution: gate → idempotency → transport → parse.
2: //!
3: //! Shared by post/reply/quote (3.4.3) and the engagement/management writes
4: //! (3.4.4). Every write flows through:
5: //! 1. `twr_core::decide` (§5.3 table) — Preview/Execute/Prompt/Deny.
6: //! 2. Idempotency pre-check (replay cached / refuse unknown).
7: //! 3. GraphQL POST (or 1.1 friendships form-POST for follow/unfollow).
8: //! 4. `_write_delay` jitter (1.5–4s, mirrors Python).
9: //!
10: //! Media upload itself is bead 3.4.5 — here `-i` paths are validated
11: //! (exist + ≤5MB + jpeg/png/gif/webp) and recorded as pending; the actual
12: //! INIT→APPEND→FINALIZE lands with the upload bead.
13: 
14: use twr_core::{decide, ApplyInput};
15: 
16: /// Max attached images per post (plan §1.1).
17: pub const MAX_IMAGES: usize = 4;
18: /// Max image bytes (5MB; chunked GIF to 15MB lands in 3.4.5).
19: pub const MAX_IMAGE_BYTES: u64 = 5 * 1024 * 1024;
20: 
21: const IMAGE_EXTS: &[&str] = &["jpg", "jpeg", "png", "gif", "webp"];
22: 
23: /// Validate `-i` image paths without uploading. Returns the paths when all
24: /// exist, are files, have image extensions, and fit in 5MB.
25: pub fn validate_images(paths: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<String>, String> {
26:     if paths.len() > MAX_IMAGES {
27:         return Err(format!(
28:             "at most {MAX_IMAGES} images per post (got {})",
29:             paths.len()
30:         ));
31:     }
32:     for p in paths {
33:         let path = std::path::Path::new(p);
34:         let ext = path
35:             .extension()
36:             .and_then(|e| e.to_str())
37:             .map(|e| e.to_lowercase())
38:             .unwrap_or_default();
39:         if !IMAGE_EXTS.contains(&ext.as_str()) {
40:             return Err(format!("unsupported image type: {p} (jpeg/png/gif/webp)"));
41:         }
42:         let meta = std::fs::metadata(path).map_err(|_| format!("cannot read image file: {p}"))?;
43:         if !meta.is_file() {
44:             return Err(format!("not a file: {p}"));
45:         }
46:         if meta.len() > MAX_IMAGE_BYTES {
47:             return Err(format!("image over 5MB: {p} (chunked GIF lands in 3.4.5)"));
48:         }
49:     }
50:     Ok(paths.to_vec())
51: }
52: 
53: /// Variables for CreateTweet (post / reply / quote / edit share the op).
54: /// `edit_target`: Some(id) adds `edit_options: {previous_tweet_id}` —
55: /// the twikit mechanism (same op + endpoint, NOT a separate EditTweet
56: /// mutation; see bead o1l.4.2 comment). `card_uri`: poll card passthrough
57: /// (twikit `poll_uri` → `card_uri`; bead o1l.4.3 comment).
58: /// Thin wrapper kept for the existing unit test + any plain-path callers.
59: #[allow(dead_code)]
60: pub fn create_tweet_vars(
61:     text: &str,
62:     reply_to: Option<&str>,
63:     quote_url: Option<&str>,
64:     media_ids: &[String],
65: ) -> serde_json::Value {
66:     create_tweet_vars_full(text, reply_to, quote_url, media_ids, None, None)
67: }
68: 
69: /// Full variant with edit + card_uri (edit/poll beads).
70: pub fn create_tweet_vars_full(
71:     text: &str,
72:     reply_to: Option<&str>,
73:     quote_url: Option<&str>,
74:     media_ids: &[String],
75:     card_uri: Option<&str>,
76:     edit_target: Option<&str>,
77: ) -> serde_json::Value {
78:     let media_entities: Vec<serde_json::Value> = media_ids
79:         .iter()
80:         .map(|mid| serde_json::json!({"media_id": mid, "tagged_users": []}))
81:         .collect();
82:     let mut vars = serde_json::json!({
83:         "tweet_text": text,
84:         "media": {"media_entities": media_entities, "possibly_sensitive": false},
85:         "semantic_annotation_ids": [],
86:         "dark_request": false,
87:     });
88:     if let Some(reply_id) = reply_to {
89:         vars["reply"] = serde_json::json!({
90:             "in_reply_to_tweet_id": reply_id,
91:             "exclude_reply_user_ids": [],
92:         });
93:     }
94:     if let Some(url) = quote_url {
95:         vars["attachment_url"] = serde_json::json!(url);
96:     }
97:     if let Some(uri) = card_uri {
98:         vars["card_uri"] = serde_json::json!(uri);
99:     }
100:     if let Some(prev) = edit_target {
101:         vars["edit_options"] = serde_json::json!({"previous_tweet_id": prev});
102:     }
103:     vars
104: }
105: 
106: /// Variables for PinTweet/UnpinTweet: `{"tweet_id"}` (deck GraphQL.json
107: /// capture; same bare shape as the favorite/bookmark ops).
108: pub fn pin_vars(tweet_id: &str) -> serde_json::Value {
109:     serde_json::json!({"tweet_id": tweet_id})
110: }
111: 
112: /// Standard-post weighted-length threshold: X's rule is 280 weighted
113: /// characters (twitter-text weighting: URLs count 23 chars regardless of
114: /// length). twr does NOT vendor twitter-text: this counts Unicode scalar
115: /// values with URL normalization (http/https links → 23), which matches
116: /// twitter-text v3 for the cases that matter here (ASCII + CJK + emoji +
117: /// URLs) and can only differ on exotic-case weights. Over-threshold text
118: /// routes to CreateNoteTweet; at-or-under stays on CreateTweet (mirrors
119: /// upstream PR #64's routing test). Conservative direction: a false
120: /// "over" only sends a Premium-gated op that fails cleanly with exit 4,
121: /// while a false "under" would 186-fail a post that should have routed.
122: pub const NOTE_TWEET_THRESHOLD: usize = 280;
123: 
124: /// Weighted length of tweet text: URLs → 23 chars each, everything else →
125: /// one unit per Unicode scalar value.
126: pub fn weighted_length(text: &str) -> usize {
127:     let mut len = 0;
128:     let mut rest = text;
129:     while let Some(pos) = rest.find("http://").or_else(|| rest.find("https://")) {
130:         len += rest[..pos].chars().count();
131:         let after = &rest[pos..];
132:         let url_len = after
133:             .split_whitespace()
134:             .next()
135:             .map(|u| {
136:                 // Trim trailing punctuation the counter wouldn't include.
137:                 u.trim_end_matches([',', '.', '!', '?', ';', ':', ')', ']', '"', '\''])
138:                     .len()
139:             })
140:             .unwrap_or(0);
141:         len += 23;
142:         rest = &after[url_len.min(after.len())..];
143:     }
144:     len + rest.chars().count()
145: }
146: 
147: /// True when text must route to CreateNoteTweet (over-threshold).
148: /// Reply/quote bodies use the SAME threshold (upstream PR #64's lesson:
149: /// quote_tweet must route too, not just create_tweet).
150: pub fn needs_note_tweet(text: &str) -> bool {
151:     weighted_length(text) > NOTE_TWEET_THRESHOLD
152: }
153: 
154: /// Variables for CreateNoteTweet: 1-1 from Rettiwt-API `postNote` VERBATIM
155: /// (pinned blob 4f11105, src/requests/Tweet.ts; full body in bead
156: /// o1l.4.1's comment; independently fetched + verified by c1).
157: /// `disallowed_reply_options: null` is LOAD-BEARING (omitting it is the
158: /// silent-empty-tweet_results failure mode). NO `dark_request` (CreateTweet
159: /// has it, NoteTweet does not). `media`: MediaVariable-shaped when images
160: /// exist, else UNDEFINED (Rust: omit the key — `None`, not null).
161: /// NO `queryId`-in-body, NO `fieldToggles` (do NOT copy the list-mutation
162: /// or CreateTweet patterns here). Reply/quote long-form vars UNCONFIRMED
163: /// (postNote has no reply/quote path) — the router sends plain-post shape
164: /// only; long quote/reply FAILS CLOSED below instead of guessing.
165: pub fn note_tweet_vars(
166:     text: &str,
167:     media_ids: &[String],
168:     reply_to: Option<&str>,
169:     quote_id: Option<&str>,
170: ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
171:     if reply_to.is_some() || quote_id.is_some() {
172:         return Err(
173:             "long-form reply/quote variables are UNCONFIRMED (Rettiwt postNote has no reply/quote path) — refusing to guess; shorten the text or drop the reply/quote target"
174:                 .to_string(),
175:         );
176:     }
177:     let mut vars = serde_json::json!({
178:         "tweet_text": text,
179:         "semantic_annotation_ids": [],
180:         "disallowed_reply_options": null,
181:     });
182:     if !media_ids.is_empty() {
183:         let entities: Vec<serde_json::Value> = media_ids
184:             .iter()
185:             .map(|mid| serde_json::json!({"media_id": mid, "tagged_users": []}))
186:             .collect();
187:         vars["media"] = serde_json::json!({
188:             "media_entities": entities,
189:             "possibly_sensitive": false,
190:         });
191:     }
192:     Ok(vars)
193: }
194: 
195: /// Classify an edit rejection message into (code, suggestion), all
196: /// exit-4 / retryable:false (o1l.4.2 taxonomy). Message-substring based —
197: /// X has no stable numeric code per case. Returns None when the message
198: /// matches none of the three known cases (caller falls through to the
199: /// generic from_api_code path).
200: pub fn classify_edit_rejection(message: &str) -> Option<(&'static str, &'static str)> {
201:     let m = message.to_lowercase();
202:     if m.contains("not eligible")
203:         || m.contains("premium")
204:         || m.contains("blue")
205:         || m.contains("subscri")
206:     {
207:         Some((
208:             "edit-not-eligible",
209:             "tweet editing needs X Premium on this account; delete-and-repost instead, or give up",
210:         ))
211:     } else if m.contains("window")
212:         || m.contains("expired")
213:         || m.contains("too old")
214:         || m.contains("editable_until")
215:     {
216:         Some((
217:             "edit-window-expired",
218:             "the edit window for this tweet has passed; delete-and-repost instead — retrying will never succeed",
219:         ))
220:     } else if m.contains("count")
221:         || m.contains("exhausted")
222:         || m.contains("limit")
223:         || m.contains("no more edits")
224:         || m.contains("locked")
225:     {
226:         Some((
227:             "edit-count-exhausted",
228:             "this tweet has no edits remaining; delete-and-repost instead — retrying will never succeed",
229:         ))
230:     } else {
231:         None
232:     }
233: }
234: 
235: /// Parse a CreateNoteTweet response's tweet id across the three envelope
236: /// shapes seen in the wild (`create_tweet` / `notetweet_create` /
237: /// `create_note_tweet`). Returns None when NO shape confirms — the caller
238: /// FAILS CLOSED (explicit error, never false-success; PR #65 lesson).
239: pub fn parse_note_tweet_id(payload: &serde_json::Value) -> Option<String> {
240:     for pointer in [
241:         "/data/create_tweet/tweet_results/result/rest_id",
242:         "/data/notetweet_create/notetweet_results/result/rest_id",
243:         "/data/notetweet_create/tweet_results/result/rest_id",
244:         "/data/create_note_tweet/tweet_results/result/rest_id",
245:         "/data/create_note_tweet/notetweet_results/result/rest_id",
246:     ] {
247:         if let Some(id) = payload
248:             .pointer(pointer)
249:             .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
250:             .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
251:         {
252:             return Some(id.to_string());
253:         }
254:     }
255:     None
256: }
257: 
258: /// Variables for CreateList: `{name, description?, isPrivate?}`.
259: /// Sources (BOTH fetched verbatim 2026-09-16, keys agree exactly):
260: /// - Rettiwt-API `List.create` (pinned blob 4f11105, src/requests/List.ts;
261: ///   cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/rettiwt-api@7.1.3/src/requests/List.ts):
262: ///   `{isPrivate, name, ...(description?)}` — description conditionally
263: ///   spread (omitted when undefined).
264: /// - twikit `gql.create_list` (d60/twikit main, twikit/client/gql.py:516-522):
265: ///   `{isPrivate, name, description}` — description ALWAYS present (twikit's
266: ///   Client.create_list defaults it to '').
267: ///
268: /// `description` omitted when empty below (Rettiwt spread verbatim; twikit's
269: /// always-present '' is equivalent on the wire — X treats missing and ""
270: /// identically for this field per both callers working).
271: /// (The live 214's cause is still UNRESOLVED: vars keys match BOTH working
272: /// callers exactly, so the 214 is NOT a vars-keys issue — see the
273: /// queryId-gated hypothesis in `run_list_write`; features-override and
274: /// queryId-in-body fixes both failed live.)
275: pub fn create_list_vars(name: &str, description: Option<&str>, private: bool) -> serde_json::Value {
276:     let mut vars = serde_json::json!({"name": name, "isPrivate": private});
277:     if let Some(d) = description.filter(|d| !d.is_empty()) {
278:         vars["description"] = serde_json::json!(d);
279:     }
280:     vars
281: }
282: 
283: /// Variables for UpdateList: SPARSE partial update (`{listId}` + only the
284: /// fields being changed). Confirmed by BOTH working callers (fetched verbatim
285: /// 2026-09-16): Rettiwt-API `List.update`
286: /// (cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/rettiwt-api@7.1.3/src/requests/List.ts:
287: /// `{listId, ...(isPrivate?), ...(description?), ...(name?)}`) AND twikit
288: /// `gql.update_list` (d60/twikit main, twikit/client/gql.py:535-543: same
289: /// conditional-add pattern) — each field conditionally added, never
290: /// full-resend.
291: /// The CLI still requires all three flags (full-resend at the CLI layer —
292: /// never send a sparse shape that could blank a field server-side by
293: /// accident), but the WIRE shape is per-field conditional like both
294: /// references.
295: pub fn update_list_vars(
296:     list_id: &str,
297:     name: Option<&str>,
298:     description: Option<&str>,
299:     private: Option<bool>,
300: ) -> serde_json::Value {
301:     let mut vars = serde_json::json!({"listId": list_id});
302:     if let Some(p) = private {
303:         vars["isPrivate"] = serde_json::json!(p);
304:     }
305:     if let Some(d) = description {
306:         vars["description"] = serde_json::json!(d);
307:     }
308:     if let Some(n) = name {
309:         vars["name"] = serde_json::json!(n);
310:     }
311:     vars
312: }
313: 
314: /// Variables for DeleteList / ListSubscribe / ListUnsubscribe /
315: /// UpdatePinnedTimelines-by-id: bare `{listId}` (deck op names confirmed;
316: /// exact key casing `listId` matches Rettiwt `List.delete/mute/unmute`
317: /// (`{listId: id}` verbatim, cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/rettiwt-api@7.1.3) and
318: /// twikit banner/member mutations (`{'listId': list_id}`, gql.py:524-557) —
319: /// live-verify on first real call for Subscribe/Unsubscribe/PinnedTimelines,
320: /// whose vars are deck-inferred, not caller-confirmed).
321: pub fn list_id_vars(list_id: &str) -> serde_json::Value {
322:     serde_json::json!({"listId": list_id})
323: }
324: 
325: /// Variables for ListAddMember/ListRemoveMember: `{listId, userId}` — ONE
326: /// user per invocation, no batch flag (ban-risk rule, plan §13.3: bulk
327: /// list-adds are a spam-report vector; looping belongs in the orchestrator,
328: /// not in twr). Keys confirmed by BOTH working callers (fetched verbatim
329: /// 2026-09-16): Rettiwt `List.addMember/removeMember`
330: /// (`{listId, userId}`, cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/rettiwt-api@7.1.3) AND twikit
331: /// `gql.list_add_member/list_remove_member`
332: /// (`{'listId': ..., 'userId': ...}`, d60/twikit main, gql.py:545-557).
333: pub fn list_member_vars(list_id: &str, user_id: &str) -> serde_json::Value {
334:     serde_json::json!({"listId": list_id, "userId": user_id})
335: }
336: 
337: /// Per-op `variables` for the engagement ops, mirroring the Python
338: /// `client.py` shapes exactly (live-fixed 2026-09-16): `DeleteRetweet`
339: /// takes `{"source_tweet_id"}` NOT `{"tweet_id"}` (a real 400-class server
340: /// rejection mis-surfaced as exit-6 otherwise); `CreateRetweet` and the
341: /// *unlike* path take `{"tweet_id","dark_request":false}`; plain
342: /// favorite/bookmark ops take a bare `{"tweet_id"}`.
343: pub fn tweet_id_vars(op: &str, tweet_id: &str) -> serde_json::Value {
344:     match op {
345:         "DeleteRetweet" => serde_json::json!({"source_tweet_id": tweet_id, "dark_request": false}),
346:         "CreateRetweet" | "DeleteTweet" | "UnfavoriteTweet" => {
347:             serde_json::json!({"tweet_id": tweet_id, "dark_request": false})
348:         }
349:         _ => serde_json::json!({"tweet_id": tweet_id}),
350:     }
351: }
352: 
353: /// Jittered post-write delay bounds (mirrors `_write_delay` 1.5–4s).
354: /// `u01` is a caller-supplied uniform in [0,1) so tests can pin it.
355: pub fn write_delay_secs(u01: f64) -> f64 {
356:     1.5 + 2.5 * u01.clamp(0.0, 1.0)
357: }
358: 
359: /// Resolve the §5.3 gate for a write invocation. `prompt_yes` is the TTY
360: /// answer when the table says Prompt (true=yes→execute). Returns the
361: /// [`twr_core::Decision`] so callers branch uniformly.
362: pub fn gate(
363:     apply: bool,
364:     dry_run: bool,
365:     no_interactive: bool,
366:     stdin_is_tty: bool,
367: ) -> twr_core::Decision {
368:     decide(&ApplyInput {
369:         apply,
370:         dry_run,
371:         no_interactive,
372:         stdin_is_tty,
373:     })
374: }

Thinking
Now I have a good understanding of the write.rs module. It covers all the write operations:
- CreateTweet (post/reply/quote/edit)
- CreateNoteTweet (long-form post)
- CreateList, UpdateList, DeleteList, ListAddMember, ListRemoveMember
- ListSubscribe, ListUnsubscribe, UpdatePinnedTimelines
- PinTweet, UnpinTweet
- Like/Favorite, Unlike
- CreateRetweet, DeleteRetweet
- Bookmark, Unbookmark
- Follow, Unfollow
- Mute, Unmute

3899: // ── engagement/management writes (bead 3.4.4) ────────────────────────────
3900: 
3901: /// Operation descriptor for the engagement writes.
3902: struct EngageOp {
3903:     op: &'static str,
3904:     use_friendships: bool,
3905: }
3906: 
3907: /// 1.1 REST path for one mute/block subcommand: mute rides
3908: /// `mutes/users/create|destroy`, block rides `blocks/create|destroy`
3909: /// (deck v1.1.json; form-POST like follow/unfollow). There is NO GraphQL
3910: /// MuteUser/BlockUser op in any reference — do not add one.
3911: fn mute_block_rest_path(cmd: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
3912:     match cmd {
3913:         "mute" => Some("mutes/users/create"),
3914:         "unmute" => Some("mutes/users/destroy"),
3915:         "block" => Some("blocks/create"),
3916:         "unblock" => Some("blocks/destroy"),
3917:         _ => None,
3918:     }
3919: }
3920: 
3921: /// Map subcommand to GraphQL op (or 1.1 friendships endpoint).
3922: fn engage_op_of(cmd: &str) -> EngageOp {
3923:     match cmd {
3924:         "pin" => EngageOp {
3925:             op: "PinTweet",
3926:             use_friendships: false,
3927:         },
3928:         "unpin" => EngageOp {
3929:             op: "UnpinTweet",
3930:             use_friendships: false,
3931:         },
3932:         "delete" => EngageOp {
3933:             op: "DeleteTweet",
3934:             use_friendships: false,
3935:         },
3936:         "like" => EngageOp {
3937:             op: "FavoriteTweet",
3938:             use_friendships: false,
3939:         },
3940:         "unlike" => EngageOp {
3941:             op: "UnfavoriteTweet",
3942:             use_friendships: false,
3943:         },
3944:         "retweet" => EngageOp {
3945:             op: "CreateRetweet",
3946:             use_friendships: false,
3947:         },
3948:         "unretweet" => EngageOp {
3949:             op: "DeleteRetweet",
3950:             use_friendships: false,
3951:         },
3952:         "bookmark" => EngageOp {
3953:             op: "CreateBookmark",
3954:             use_friendships: false,
3955:         },
3956:         "unbookmark" => EngageOp {
3957:             op: "DeleteBookmark",
3958:             use_friendships: false,
3959:         },
3960:         "follow" => EngageOp {
3961:             op: "friendships/create",
3962:             use_friendships: true,
3963:         },
3964:         _ => EngageOp {
3965:             op: "friendships/destroy",
3966:             use_friendships: true,
3967:         },
3968:     }
3969: }

Now I see the issue. At line 4127:
```rust
let url = format!("https://x.com/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{}", desc.op);
```

And at line 4140-4143:
```rust
let vars = if desc.op == "PinTweet" || desc.op == "UnpinTweet" {
    cli::write::pin_vars(&target_id)
} else {
    cli::write::tweet_id_vars(desc.op, &target_id)
};
```

The `tweet_id_vars` function at line 343-351 of write.rs handles:
- "DeleteRetweet" → `{"source_tweet_id": tweet_id, "dark_request": false}`
- "CreateRetweet" | "DeleteTweet" | "UnfavoriteTweet" → `{"tweet_id": tweet_id, "dark_request": false}`
- Everything else → `{"tweet_id": tweet_id}`

So for "CreateRetweet", the vars would be `{"tweet_id": "2101573073053040842", "dark_request": false}`.

And for "CreateBookmark" (which is not in the special cases), the vars would be `{"tweet_id": "2101573073053040842"}`.

- CreateRetweet: `ojPdsZsimiJrUGLR1sjUtA`
- CreateBookmark: `aoDbu3RHznuiSkQ9aNM67Q`

Wait, actually, the 404 might be because the URL path includes the operation name. The URL is:
`https://x.com/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{op}`

For example: `https://x.com/i/api/graphql/ojPdsZsimiJrUGLR1sjUtA/CreateRetweet`

If X returns 404, it could mean:
1. The query ID is wrong (X doesn't recognize it)
2. The operation name in the URL path doesn't match the query ID

155: /// Multiple entries per op accumulate (rotation order = listed order).
156: ///
157: /// NOTE: the shipped EXTRA lists are FALLBACK rotation entries, only tried

161: /// and drift detection keep working against the shipped default.
162: pub fn seeded_extra_rotation() -> crate::ExtraRotation {
163:     let mut extra = crate::ExtraRotation::new();
164:     for (op, qid) in EXTRA_FALLBACK_IDS {

173: pub fn fallback_query_id(operation: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
174:     FALLBACK_QUERY_IDS
175:         .iter()

180: /// The 20 default feature flags from `graphql.py::_DEFAULT_FEATURES` (the plan

READcrates/twr/src/cli/exec.rs
Read 1/1 file, 418 lines
1: //! Read-command execution: transport → parse → envelope data.
2: //!
3: //! One function per command family, all sharing [`ExecCtx`] (auth, transport,
4: //! query IDs, features, transaction proof, throttle, config). Pure orchestration:
5: //! build variables → pick GET/POST → headers (+ gated tx-id) → parse bytes →
6: //! `(data, pagination)`. Error mapping goes through `twr-core` kinds.
7: 
8: use twr_client::{fetch_timeline, Page, PageError, Throttle};
9: use twr_graphql::{compact_features, ExtraRotation};
10: use twr_tx::RequestProof;
11: 
12: /// Shared execution context for all read commands.
13: pub struct ExecCtx<'a> {
14:     pub transport: &'a dyn twr_client::HttpTransport,
15:     pub creds: twr_client::Credentials,
16:     pub throttle: Throttle,
17:     pub extra_rotation: ExtraRotation,
18:     pub disk_cache: twr_graphql::cache::QueryIdCache,
19:     pub now_secs: u64,
20:     pub max_count: usize,
21:     #[allow(dead_code)]
22:     pub request_delay_secs: f64,
23:     pub chrome_major: String,
24:     pub locale: String,
25:     pub tx: Option<TxState>,
26: }
27: 
28: /// Live transaction-proof state (ingredients resolved once per invocation).
29: pub struct TxState {
30:     pub inner: twr_tx::ClientTransactionV1,
31: }
32: 
33: impl<'a> ExecCtx<'a> {
34:     /// Resolve one op's query ID through the 4 layers.
35:     pub fn query_id(&self, operation: &str) -> Option<twr_graphql::ResolvedQid> {
36:         twr_graphql::resolve(
37:             operation,
38:             |name| std::env::var(name).ok(),
39:             &self.disk_cache,
40:             &self.extra_rotation,
41:             self.now_secs,
42:         )
43:     }
44: 
45:     /// Mint a transaction id for gated ops, `None` for ungated ones.
46:     pub fn proof_for(&self, operation: &str, method: &str, path: &str) -> Option<String> {
47:         self.tx
48:             .as_ref()
49:             .and_then(|tx| tx.inner.prepare(operation, method, path))
50:     }
51: }
52: 
53: /// GraphQL GET URL: `/i/api/graphql/<qid>/<op>?variables=..&features=..`.
54: pub fn graphql_get_url(
55:     query_id: &str,
56:     operation: &str,
57:     variables: &serde_json::Value,
58:     field_toggles: Option<&serde_json::Value>,
59: ) -> String {
60:     let features = serde_json::Value::Object(compact_features(operation));
61:     let mut url = format!(
62:         "https://x.com/i/api/graphql/{query_id}/{operation}?variables={}&features={}",
63:         url_encode(&variables.to_string()),
64:         url_encode(&features.to_string()),
65:     );
66:     if let Some(toggles) = field_toggles {
67:         url.push_str(&format!(
68:             "&fieldToggles={}",
69:             url_encode(&toggles.to_string())
70:         ));
71:     }
72:     url
73: }
74: 
75: fn url_encode(s: &str) -> String {
76:     let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
77:     for b in s.bytes() {
78:         if b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"-_.~".contains(&b) {
79:             out.push(b as char);
80:         } else {
81:             out.push_str(&format!("%{b:02X}"));
82:         }
83:     }
84:     out
85: }
86: 
87: /// Fetch one timeline page over the transport and parse it with `twr-model`.
88: /// Returns `(tweets, next_cursor)`. Transport failures map to `Fatal`;
89: /// callers map HTTP statuses (429/404/...) via `twr-core` kinds.
90: pub async fn fetch_parsed_page(
91:     ctx: &mut ExecCtx<'_>,
92:     operation: &str,
93:     variables: serde_json::Value,
94:     instructions: fn(&serde_json::Value) -> Option<&Vec<serde_json::Value>>,
95: ) -> Result<(Vec<twr_model::Tweet>, Option<String>), PageError> {
96:     fetch_parsed_page_with_toggles(ctx, operation, variables, instructions, None).await
97: }
98: 
99: /// Extractor: response body -> timeline instructions (per-op deep-get path).
100: pub type InstructionsFn = fn(&serde_json::Value) -> Option<&Vec<serde_json::Value>>;
101: 
102: /// Page parser: `(body, instructions) -> (items, cursor)`.
103: pub type PageParser<T> = fn(&serde_json::Value, InstructionsFn) -> (Vec<T>, Option<String>);
104: 
105: /// Generic single-page fetch: same transport/throttle/headers/status mapping
106: /// as [`fetch_parsed_page`], but the caller supplies the parser.
107: /// Lets non-tweet timelines (lists, list members) reuse the whole transport
108: /// layer without duplicating it — the tweet-specific wrapper below is one
109: /// call of this.
110: pub async fn fetch_parsed_page_generic<T>(
111:     ctx: &mut ExecCtx<'_>,
112:     operation: &str,
113:     variables: serde_json::Value,
114:     instructions: InstructionsFn,
115:     parse: PageParser<T>,
116: ) -> Result<(Vec<T>, Option<String>), PageError> {
117:     let qid = ctx
118:         .query_id(operation)
119:         .map(|r| r.query_id)
120:         .unwrap_or_default();
121:     let use_post = twr_client::use_post(operation);
122:     let method = if use_post { "POST" } else { "GET" };
123:     let path = format!("/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{operation}");
124: 
125:     let wait = ctx.throttle.wait_secs(operation, now_f64());
126:     if wait > 0.0 {
127:         tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(wait)).await;
128:     }
129: 
130:     let tid = ctx.proof_for(operation, method, &path);
131:     let headers = twr_client::build_headers(&twr_client::HeaderInput {
132:         creds: &ctx.creds,
133:         method,
134:         os: twr_client::Os::current(),
135:         chrome_major: &ctx.chrome_major,
136:         locale: &ctx.locale,
137:         transaction_id: tid.as_deref(),
138:     });
139:     let header_refs: Vec<(&str, &str)> = headers
140:         .iter()
141:         .map(|(k, v)| (k.as_str(), v.as_str()))
142:         .collect();
143: 
144:     let resp = if use_post {
145:         let url = format!("https://x.com/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{operation}");
146:         let mut body = serde_json::Map::new();
147:         body.insert("variables".into(), variables);
148:         body.insert(
149:             "features".into(),
150:             serde_json::Value::Object(compact_features(operation)),
151:         );
152:         let raw = serde_json::to_vec(&body).unwrap_or_default();
153:         ctx.transport
154:             .post_json(&url, &header_refs, &raw)
155:             .await
156:             .map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?
157:     } else {
158:         let url = graphql_get_url(&qid, operation, &variables, None);
159:         ctx.transport
160:             .get(&url, &header_refs)
161:             .await
162:             .map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?
163:     };
164: 
165:     if resp.status == 429 {
166:         return Err(PageError::RateLimited);
167:     }
168:     if resp.status == 404 || (500..600).contains(&resp.status) {
169:         return Err(PageError::Fatal);
170:     }
171:     let body: serde_json::Value =
172:         serde_json::from_slice(&resp.body).map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?;
173:     Ok(parse(&body, instructions))
174: }
175: 
176: /// Toggle-aware variant (TweetDetail needs fieldToggles; others pass None).
177: #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
178: pub async fn fetch_parsed_page_with_toggles(
179:     ctx: &mut ExecCtx<'_>,
180:     operation: &str,
181:     variables: serde_json::Value,
182:     instructions: fn(&serde_json::Value) -> Option<&Vec<serde_json::Value>>,
183:     field_toggles: Option<serde_json::Value>,
184: ) -> Result<(Vec<twr_model::Tweet>, Option<String>), PageError> {
185:     let qid = ctx
186:         .query_id(operation)
187:         .map(|r| r.query_id)
188:         .unwrap_or_default();
189:     let use_post = twr_client::use_post(operation);
190:     let method = if use_post { "POST" } else { "GET" };
191:     let path = format!("/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{operation}");
192: 
193:     // Throttle per endpoints.yaml rps/burst (best-effort wait).
194:     let wait = ctx.throttle.wait_secs(operation, now_f64());
195:     if wait > 0.0 {
196:         tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(wait)).await;
197:     }
198: 
199:     let tid = ctx.proof_for(operation, method, &path);
200:     let headers = twr_client::build_headers(&twr_client::HeaderInput {
201:         creds: &ctx.creds,
202:         method,
203:         os: twr_client::Os::current(),
204:         chrome_major: &ctx.chrome_major,
205:         locale: &ctx.locale,
206:         transaction_id: tid.as_deref(),
207:     });
208:     let header_refs: Vec<(&str, &str)> = headers
209:         .iter()
210:         .map(|(k, v)| (k.as_str(), v.as_str()))
211:         .collect();
212: 
213:     let resp = if use_post {
214:         let url = format!("https://x.com/i/api/graphql/{qid}/{operation}");
215:         let mut body = serde_json::Map::new();
216:         body.insert("variables".into(), variables);
217:         body.insert(
218:             "features".into(),
219:             serde_json::Value::Object(compact_features(operation)),
220:         );
221:         let raw = serde_json::to_vec(&body).unwrap_or_default();
222:         ctx.transport
223:             .post_json(&url, &header_refs, &raw)
224:             .await
225:             .map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?
226:     } else {
227:         let url = graphql_get_url(&qid, operation, &variables, field_toggles.as_ref());
228:         ctx.transport
229:             .get(&url, &header_refs)
230:             .await
231:             .map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?
232:     };
233: 
234:     if resp.status == 429 {
235:         return Err(PageError::RateLimited);
236:     }
237:     if resp.status == 404 || (500..600).contains(&resp.status) {
238:         return Err(PageError::Fatal);
239:     }
240:     let body: serde_json::Value =
241:         serde_json::from_slice(&resp.body).map_err(|_| PageError::Fatal)?;
242:     // Payload-shape 404 disambiguation lives in twr-core; an *Unavailable
243:     // payload here parses to zero tweets (empty page), and the CLI layer
244:     // maps that to exit 3 when the whole result is empty.
245:     let (tweets, cursor) = twr_model::parse_timeline_response(&body, instructions);
246:     Ok((tweets, cursor))
247: }
248: 
249: /// Drive the shared [`fetch_timeline`] loop over [`fetch_parsed_page`],
250: /// collecting full [`twr_model::Tweet`]s with id dedup across pages.
251: pub async fn fetch_tweets_paged(
252:     ctx: &mut ExecCtx<'_>,
253:     operation: &str,
254:     count: usize,
255:     start_cursor: Option<String>,
256:     base_variables: serde_json::Value,
257:     instructions: fn(&serde_json::Value) -> Option<&Vec<serde_json::Value>>,
258: ) -> Result<(Vec<twr_model::Tweet>, twr_client::TimelineResult), PageError> {
259:     // The loop works on IDs; we keep a side table id -> tweet.
260:     let mut by_id: std::collections::HashMap<String, twr_model::Tweet> =
261:         std::collections::HashMap::new();
262:     let mut order: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
263: 
264:     // Wrap: each loop iteration fetches one page and registers its tweets.
265:     let ctx_ref = &mut *ctx;
266:     let result = fetch_timeline(
267:         count,
268:         ctx_ref.max_count,
269:         start_cursor,
270:         true,
271:         |page_count, cursor| {
272:             let mut vars = base_variables.clone();
273:             vars["count"] = serde_json::json!(page_count);
274:             if let Some(c) = cursor {
275:                 vars["cursor"] = serde_json::json!(c);
276:             }
277:             // fetch_parsed_page is async; the loop is sync — block on it.
278:             // (Read commands run inside a tokio runtime; block_in_place
279:             // avoids nested-runtime panics.)
280:             let (tweets, next) = tokio::task::block_in_place(|| {
281:                 tokio::runtime::Handle::current().block_on(fetch_parsed_page(
282:                     ctx_ref,
283:                     operation,
284:                     vars,
285:                     instructions,
286:                 ))
287:             })?;
288:             let mut page = Page {
289:                 ids: Vec::with_capacity(tweets.len()),
290:                 next_cursor: next,
291:             };
292:             for t in tweets {
293:                 // Jittered inter-request delay is applied by the caller
294:                 // between loop iterations via request_delay_secs; the
295:                 // per-page sleep here keeps the port faithful but tiny.
296:                 page.ids.push(t.id.clone());
297:                 by_id.entry(t.id.clone()).or_insert(t);
298:             }
299:             // Track order of first appearance for stable output.
300:             for id in &page.ids {
301:                 if !order.contains(id) {
302:                     order.push(id.clone());
303:                 }
304:             }
305:             Ok(page)
306:         },
307:     )?;
308: 
309:     let mut tweets = Vec::new();
310:     for id in result.ids.iter() {
311:         if let Some(t) = by_id.remove(id) {
312:             tweets.push(t);
313:         }
314:     }
315:     // Preserve loop order (deduped).
316:     Ok((tweets, result))
317: }
318: 
319: pub fn now_f64() -> f64 {
320:     std::time::SystemTime::now()
321:         .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
322:         .map(|d| d.as_secs_f64())
323:         .unwrap_or(0.0)
324: }
325: 
326: /// Best-effort per-op throttle wait (bead o1l.6): consult the token bucket
327: /// built from `endpoints.yaml` rps/burst, sleep when it says to. Mutations
328: /// are one-shot (no paging loop), so without this the yaml entries for new
329: /// mutation ops would be dead config. Never fatal: a zero wait fires
330: /// immediately, and the sleep only ever delays, never fails.
331: pub async fn throttle_wait(throttle: &mut Throttle, operation: &str) {
332:     let wait = throttle.wait_secs(operation, now_f64());
333:     if wait > 0.0 {
334:         tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(wait)).await;
335:     }
336: }

345:     /// key silently drops the op back to the default, and this test catches
346:     /// exactly that drift. Keys are the runner-side names (CLI op for
347:     /// run_engage's 1.1-REST rides, GraphQL op for post/list writes, DmSend

class GQLClient:
    def __init__(self, base: ClientType) -> None:
        self.base = base

    async def gql_get(
        self,
        url: str,
        variables: dict,
        features: dict | None = None,
        headers: dict | None = None,
        extra_params: dict | None = None,
        **kwargs
    ):
        params = {'variables': variables}
        if features is not None:
            params['features'] = features
        if extra_params is not None:
            params |= extra_params
        if headers is None:
            headers = self.base._base_headers
        return await self.base.get(url, params=flatten_params(params), headers=headers, **kwargs)

    async def gql_post(
        self,
        url: str,
        variables: dict,
        features: dict | None = None,
        headers: dict | None = None,
        extra_data: dict | None = None,
        **kwargs
    ):
        data = {'variables': variables, 'queryId': get_query_id(url)}
        if features is not None:
            data['features'] = features
        if extra_data is not None:
            data |= extra_data
        if headers is None:
            headers = self.base._base_headers
        return await self.base.post(url, json=data, headers=headers, **kwargs)

    async def search_timeline(
        self,
        query: str,
        product: str,
        count: int,
        cursor: str | None
    ):
        variables = {
            'rawQuery': query,
            'count': count,
            'querySource': 'typed_query',
            'product': product
        }
        if cursor is not None:
            variables['cursor'] = cursor
        return await self.gql_get(Endpoint.SEARCH_TIMELINE, variables, FEATURES)

    async def similar_posts(self, tweet_id: str):
        variables = {'tweet_id': tweet_id}
        return await self.gql_get(
            Endpoint.SIMILAR_POSTS,
            variables,
            SIMILAR_POSTS_FEATURES
        )

    async def create_tweet(
        self, is_note_tweet, text, media_entities,
        poll_uri, reply_to, attachment_url,
        community_id, share_with_followers,
        richtext_options, edit_tweet_id, limit_mode
    ):
        variables = {
            'tweet_text': text,
            'dark_request': False,
            'media': {
                'media_entities': media_entities,
                'possibly_sensitive': False
            },
            'semantic_annotation_ids': [],
        }

        if poll_uri is not None:
            variables['card_uri'] = poll_uri

        if reply_to is not None:
            variables['reply'] = {
                'in_reply_to_tweet_id': reply_to,
                'exclude_reply_user_ids': []
            }

        if limit_mode is not None:
            variables['conversation_control'] = {'mode': limit_mode}

        if attachment_url is not None:
            variables['attachment_url'] = attachment_url

        if community_id is not None:
            variables['semantic_annotation_ids'] = [{
                'entity_id': community_id,
                'group_id': '8',
                'domain_id': '31'
            }]
            variables['broadcast'] = share_with_followers

        if richtext_options is not None:
            is_note_tweet = True
            variables['richtext_options'] = {
                'richtext_tags': richtext_options
            }
        if edit_tweet_id is not None:
            variables['edit_options'] = {
                'previous_tweet_id': edit_tweet_id
            }

        if is_note_tweet:
            endpoint = Endpoint.CREATE_NOTE_TWEET
            features = NOTE_TWEET_FEATURES
        else:
            endpoint = Endpoint.CREATE_TWEET
            features = FEATURES
        return await self.gql_post(endpoint, variables, features)

    async def create_scheduled_tweet(self, scheduled_at, text, media_ids) -> str:
        variables = {
            'post_tweet_request': {
            'auto_populate_reply_metadata': False,
            'status': text,
            'exclude_reply_user_ids': [],
            'media_ids': media_ids
            },
            'execute_at': scheduled_at
        }
        return await self.gql_post(Endpoint.CREATE_SCHEDULED_TWEET, variables)

    async def delete_tweet(self, tweet_id):
        variables = {
            'tweet_id': tweet_id,
            'dark_request': False
        }
        return await self.gql_post(Endpoint.DELETE_TWEET, variables)

```python
async def gql_post(self, url, variables, features=None, headers=None, extra_data=None, **kwargs):
    data = {'variables': variables, 'queryId': get_query_id(url)}
    if features is not None:
        data['features'] = features
    if extra_data is not None:
        data |= extra_data
```

Now I can see the issue clearly. The engagement write path at lines 4145-4150:
```rust
let mut body = serde_json::Map::new();
body.insert("variables".into(), vars);
body.insert(
    "features".into(),
    serde_json::Value::Object(twr_graphql::compact_features(desc.op)),
);
```

This sends `features` in the body, but doesn't send `queryId` in the body.

Twikit's `gql_post` ALWAYS sends `queryId` in the body and conditionally sends `features`. For engagement writes (favorite, retweet, bookmark, etc.), twikit calls `gql_post` without features, so the body is `{variables, queryId}`.

The fix: For engagement GraphQL writes, send `queryId` in the body. But should I also remove `features`?

Looking at twikit:
- For engagement writes: `{variables, queryId}` (no features)
- For CreateTweet: `{variables, queryId, features}` (with features)
- For list mutations: `{variables, queryId, features}` (with features)

So twikit ALWAYS sends `queryId` in the body. The difference is whether `features` is included.

For twr:
- Post (CreateTweet/CreateNoteTweet): sends `{variables, features}` (no queryId in body) — WORKS
- List mutations: sends `{variables, features}` (no queryId in body) — WORKS (after fixing the 214 by removing queryId)
- Engage (CreateRetweet, etc.): sends `{variables, features}` (no queryId in body) — FAILS with 404

So the pattern in twr is: don't send `queryId` in body, just `variables` and `features`. This works for CreateTweet and CreateList, but not for CreateRetweet/CreateBookmark.

The key question is: why does CreateTweet work without queryId in body, but CreateRetweet doesn't?

Hypothesis: X's API handles `queryId` in body differently for different operations. For some operations (like CreateTweet), if `queryId` is absent from the body, X uses the QID from the URL path. For other operations (like CreateRetweet), X might require `queryId` in the body, or might not accept `features` in the body.

Let me try the twikit pattern: send `queryId` in body, no `features` for engagement writes.

Actually, looking at twikit again:
```python
async def gql_post(self, url, variables, features=None, headers=None, extra_data=None, **kwargs):
    data = {'variables': variables, 'queryId': get_query_id(url)}
    if features is not None:
        data['features'] = features
```

`get_query_id(url)` extracts the QID from the URL. So `data['queryId']` is always the same QID as in the URL path. So twikit sends the QID both in the URL path AND in the body.

The difference between twr and twikit for engagement writes:
- twr: `{variables, features: {default_features}}`
- twikit: `{variables, queryId: "ojPdsZsimiJrUGLR1sjUtA"}`

twr sends `features` instead of `queryId`. Maybe X's server for CreateRetweet doesn't recognize the `features` field and returns 404? Or maybe it requires `queryId` in the body?
